# Test a component, a service and a route with the zoneless TestBed

Contents: 1. Rules · 2. A component · 3. HTTP · 4. A route · Sources

## 1. Rules (Angular tests)

- `TestBed` is zoneless by default, even with `zone.js` loaded, and applies the OnPush rules: change state through signals or `setInput`. [S49]
- Wait for the render with `await fixture.whenStable()`. Run effects with `TestBed.tick()`. [S48] [S52]
- Take the expected value from the request, never from the component code. Query the DOM as a user does: text, role, label.
- Mock only the boundaries: HTTP through `HttpTestingController`, never `HttpClient` by hand; never mock the router. [S50] [S55]
- Never assert the calls of a spy when the DOM or the result shows the behavior. [S82] Agents add mocks more often than people do. [S74]
- For time, use `vi.useFakeTimers()` and `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(ms)`, then `vi.useRealTimers()`; never `fakeAsync` in new code. [S65]
- A test fails: fix the code under test. Never change, skip or delete the test to make it pass.

## 2. A component (inputs, outputs, signals)

Wrong: the input set as a field, a forced detection, the expected text copied from the template code.

```ts
it('works', () => {
  const fixture = TestBed.createComponent(ValveStatus);
  fixture.componentInstance.isOpen = true as never;
  fixture.detectChanges();
  expect(fixture.nativeElement.innerHTML).toContain('<span class="state">');
});
```

Right:

```ts
it('shows OPEN for an open valve', async () => {
  const fixture = TestBed.createComponent(ValveStatus);

  fixture.componentRef.setInput('isOpen', true);
  await fixture.whenStable();

  expect(fixture.nativeElement.textContent).toContain('OPEN');
});
```

- An output: subscribe to it, `fixture.componentInstance.closed.subscribe(...)`, then act and assert. [S48]
- A child component: stub it with `overrideComponent`; never `NO_ERRORS_SCHEMA` everywhere. [S48]

## 3. HTTP (HttpTestingController)

```ts
beforeEach(() => {
  TestBed.configureTestingModule({ providers: [provideHttpClient(), provideHttpClientTesting()] });
});

afterEach(() => {
  TestBed.inject(HttpTestingController).verify();
});

it('reads one valve', async () => {
  const pending = firstValueFrom(TestBed.inject(ValveApi).get('V-101'));
  const request = TestBed.inject(HttpTestingController).expectOne('/api/valves/V-101');

  request.flush({ tag: 'V-101', isOpen: true });

  expect(await pending).toEqual({ tag: 'V-101', isOpen: true });
});
```

- `provideHttpClient()` comes before `provideHttpClientTesting()`. [S50]
- `httpResource`: create it in an injection context, `TestBed.tick()` to send the request, `expectOne` and `flush`, then `await TestBed.inject(ApplicationRef).whenStable()`. [S50]

## 4. A route (RouterTestingHarness)

```ts
TestBed.configureTestingModule({ providers: [provideRouter([{ path: 'valves/:tag', component: ValveDetail }])] });
const harness = await RouterTestingHarness.create();
await harness.navigateByUrl('/valves/V-101', ValveDetail);

expect(harness.routeNativeElement?.textContent).toContain('V-101');
```

[S55]

## Sources

- [S48] Angular, "Component testing scenarios", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing/components-scenarios>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S49] Angular, "Zoneless", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/zoneless>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S50] Angular, "Test requests", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/http/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S52] Angular, "TestBed", API reference, v22, <https://angular.dev/api/core/testing/TestBed>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S55] Angular, "Testing routing and navigation", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/routing/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S65] Vitest, "Vi" API reference, v5.1.0, <https://vitest.dev/api/vi>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S74] A. Hora, R. Robbes, "Are Coding Agents Generating Over-Mocked Tests? An Empirical Study", MSR 2026, arXiv:2602.00409, <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.00409v1>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S82] A. Eagle, "Testing on the Toilet: Change-Detector Tests Considered Harmful", Google Testing Blog, 2015-01-27, <https://testing.googleblog.com/2015/01/testing-on-toilet-change-detector-tests.html>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

tool: node
version: "26"

# Node.js versions and built-in APIs

Contents: 1. Versions · 2. Running a TypeScript file · 3. Built-in APIs · Sources

## 1. Versions (Node.js LTS)

| Node.js | Released | State on 2026-11-12 | Angular that accepts it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 26 | 2026-05-05 | LTS | 22 |
| 24 | 2025-05-06 | LTS | 20, 21; 22 from 24.15 |
| 22 | 2024-04-24 | LTS | 18 to 21; 22 from 22.22.3 |
| 20 | 2023-04-17 | End of life | 17 to 21 |

Use only LTS versions in production. From version 27, one major version a year, and every version becomes LTS. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Running a TypeScript file directly (Node.js type stripping)

From Node.js 22.18, `node file.ts` runs a TypeScript file with no option. Node.js replaces the types by spaces: it checks no type and ignores `tsconfig.json`. [S3]

- Refused: `enum`, a `namespace` with code, parameter properties in a constructor, decorators, `.tsx` files.
- Write the extension in each import: `import { x } from './file.ts'`. Write `import type` for a type.
- A `.ts` file under `node_modules` is refused.

Wrong:

```ts
enum Mode { Fix, Check }
import { checkBase } from './check-base';
```

Right:

```ts
const MODES = ['fix', 'check'] as const;
import { checkBase } from './check-base.ts';
```

## 3. Built-in APIs that replace packages (Node.js 22 to 26)

| API | Since | Replaces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `node --watch` | 22, stable | A tool that restarts on change |
| `glob`, `globSync` in `node:fs` | 22 | A file pattern package |
| `WebSocket` (client) | 22 | A WebSocket client package |
| `fetch`, `util.parseArgs`, `node:test` and `node --test` | 22 at most: the scripts and tests of this base use them with 22.18 | An HTTP client, an argument parser, a test runner |
| `require()` of an ES module | 22.12 | CommonJS and ES module workarounds |
| `Temporal` | 26 | A date library |

[S4] [S2] [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Node.js, "Releases", <https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Node.js, "Modules: TypeScript", v26, <https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] Node.js, "Node.js 22 is now available!", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/announcements/v22-release-announce>, and "Node.js 26.0.0", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/release/v26.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Scripts and tests of this base, run with Node.js 22.18.0 and 24.18.0 on 2026-11-13.

id: typescript-rxjs-and-signals
title: RxJS 7 and Angular signals
topic: typescript
summary: RxJS 7 deprecations, the bridge between signals and observables, and when to use each. RxJS 7.
tool: rxjs
version: "7.8"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3]
license: MIT, written for this base; facts summarized from the RxJS types (Apache 2.0) and the Angular documentation (CC BY 4.0)
variant: chat
---

# RxJS 7 and Angular signals

Contents: 1. RxJS stays at 7 · 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 · 3. Signals and observables · 4. The bridge · Sources

## 1. RxJS stays at 7 (RxJS versions)

The current version is 7.8.2. Version 8 is paused, with no release date: write RxJS 7 code. Angular 16 to 22 accept `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 (RxJS 7 deprecations)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `source$.toPromise()` | `await firstValueFrom(source$)` or `await lastValueFrom(source$)` |
| `subscribe(next, error, complete)` | `subscribe({ next, error, complete })` |
| `Observable.create(…)` | `new Observable(…)` |

[S1]

Wrong:

```ts
const valves = await this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves').toPromise();
```

Right:

```ts
const valves = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves'));
```

## 3. Signals for state, observables for streams (Angular signals and RxJS)

- State of a component, and values derived from it: signals (`signal`, `computed`). [S3]
- Events over time, cancellation, combination of streams (messages, timers, sockets): observables. [S3]
- An observable in a template: the `async` pipe, or `toSignal()` in the component.
- A simple HTTP request, from Angular 22: `httpResource()`. Before 22: `HttpClient`.

## 4. The bridge between signals and observables (rxjs-interop)

| API, from `@angular/core/rxjs-interop` | Role | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `toSignal(obs$, { initialValue })` | Observable to signal | Subscribes at once; needs the injection context, so call it in a field; without `initialValue`, gives `undefined` until the first value |
| `toObservable(signal)` | Signal to observable | Several synchronous writes give one value only |
| `rxResource({ params, stream })` | Resource fed by an observable | Same state signals as `resource` |
| `takeUntilDestroyed()` | End a subscription with the component | Without an argument, only in a field or the constructor |

[S3]

## Sources

- [S1] ReactiveX, types of `rxjs` 7.8.2 (`@deprecated` tags), <https://app.unpkg.com/rxjs@7.8.2/files/dist/types/internal/Observable.d.ts>, and the roadmap issue 6367, <https://github.com/ReactiveX/rxjs/issues/6367>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

id: typescript-typescript-changes

topic: typescript
summary: Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9, new defaults and removed options of 6.0, and what 7.0 changes. TypeScript 6.0.
tool: typescript
version: "6.0"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4]

variant: chat

# TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0: what changed

Contents: 1. Version rule · 2. Syntax from 5.0 to 5.9 · 3. TypeScript 6.0 · 4. TypeScript 7.0 · Sources

## 1. Version rule (TypeScript versions)

Read the version of `typescript` in `package.json`. Use a syntax only when that version is equal to or higher than the version that added it. [S1]

In an Angular 22 project, `typescript` stays at 6.0: Angular 22 and typescript-eslint 8 accept `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. Never move it to 7 when you update dependencies. [S2] [S4]

## 2. Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9 (TypeScript 5)

| Version | Use | Breaking change to know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5.0 | Standard decorators; `const` type parameters; `extends` with an array in `tsconfig`; `moduleResolution: bundler`; `verbatimModuleSyntax`; `export type *` | `target: ES3`, `out`, `importsNotUsedAsValues` deprecated |
| 5.2 | `using` and `await using` for disposable resources | `module` and `moduleResolution` must match for `node16` and `nodenext` |
| 5.3 | Import attributes: `import data from './a.json' with { type: 'json' }` | `super.x` on an instance field is an error |
| 5.4 | `NoInfer<T>`; narrowing kept in closures; `Object.groupBy`; `module: preserve` | Stricter assignment between enums |
| 5.5 | Inferred type predicates: `values.filter((v) => v !== undefined)` narrows the type | Options deprecated in 5.0 have no effect |
| 5.6 | Conditions always true or always false are errors; iterator helper methods | `.tsbuildinfo` always written |
| 5.8 | `erasableSyntaxOnly`: only syntax that Node.js can strip | `assert` in an import is refused under `nodenext` |
| 5.9 | `import defer`; `module: node20` | `ArrayBuffer` is no longer a supertype of `Buffer` |

[S1]

## 3. TypeScript 6.0 (released 2026-03-23)

New defaults: `strict: true`, `module: esnext`, `target` the latest ECMAScript (`es2025`), `types: []`, `rootDir` the folder of `tsconfig.json`, `noUncheckedSideEffectImports: true`. [S3]

| Old option or syntax | State in 6.0 | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `"moduleResolution": "node"` or `"node10"` | Deprecated | `bundler` or `nodenext` |
| `"moduleResolution": "classic"`, `"outFile"` | Removed | `bundler`; ES modules |
| `"baseUrl"` | Deprecated | Full paths in `paths` |
| `"target": "es5"`, `"downlevelIteration"` | Deprecated | ES2015 at least |
| `"module": "amd"`, `"umd"`, `"systemjs"`, `"none"` | Not supported | ES modules |
| `module Foo { }` | Error | `namespace Foo { }` |
| `import x from './a.json' assert { type: 'json' }` | Error | `with { type: 'json' }` |

- To silence the deprecations while you migrate: `"ignoreDeprecations": "6.0"`. Remove it once done. [S3]
- New types: `Temporal`, `Map.prototype.getOrInsert`, `RegExp.escape`. [S3]

## 4. TypeScript 7.0 (released 2026-07-08)

- The compiler is native: builds are 7 to 12 times faster. `npm install -D typescript` installs it. [S4]
- It has no programming API before 7.1: Angular and typescript-eslint cannot use it yet. [S4]
- The options deprecated in 6.0 are errors. [S4]
- The 6.0 compiler stays available as `@typescript/typescript6`, command `tsc6`. [S4]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TypeScript 5.0" to "TypeScript 5.9" release notes, <https://typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html> and the pages of the following versions, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 6.0", 2026-03-23, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-6-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 7.0", 2026-07-08, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 ed32e16109eb31eb2e13d94b61bd2f09570b919eacd85abd98c9c6c47fa25d61 RAW evaluation/README.md
# Evaluation of the base

Does the AI succeed more often with the base than without it, with no more tokens? This folder holds
the task set, the protocol and the record format. Run it on the workstation, with the real model.

## How to run the evaluation

1. Prepare two copies of the same trial project: one with the base at its root (`node import.ts
   --project`), one without.
2. For each task of the set, start a new session of the coding assistant in non-interactive mode,
   with the task text as the prompt, in each copy. Repeat each task the number of runs of the table.
3. After each run, judge success with the check of the task, and note the tokens that the assistant
   reports for the run.
4. Write one line per run in `results.csv` (format below), then run
   `node scripts/evaluation-summary.ts evaluation/results.csv`.

A small team can only see large gaps: about 40 runs per condition are needed to see a gap of
30 points. Start with tasks on APIs and versions that a model does not know well: that is where the
base changes the result.

## Task set

Fake data only. Replace or add real tasks of the team, written without real data.

| Id | Task (prompt) | Success check | Runs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Add a standalone Angular component that shows a list of valves with their position, in Angular 22. | `npm run check` and `npm run build` at zero errors; no `standalone: true`, no `*ngFor` | 4 |
| T2 | Add an input `tagName` and an output `selected` to the component, with signals. | Same checks; `input()` and `output()` used | 4 |
| T3 | Add a minimal API endpoint `GET /valves/{tagName}` with typed results and validation, in .NET 10. | `dotnet build` at zero errors; `TypedResults` used | 4 |
| T4 | Log the opening of a valve with a structured message. | `dotnet build` at zero errors (CA2254 on) | 4 |
| T5 | Write the Dockerfile of the API project. | `hadolint` clean; two stages; `USER $APP_UID`; port 8080 | 4 |
| T6 | Write the Deployment and Service of the API, for Kubernetes 1.37. | `kubeconform -strict` and `kube-linter` clean | 4 |
| T7 | Give the properties of a motorized butterfly valve, with their units. | Names from the catalog; each number ends with a suffix of the closed list | 4 |
| T8 | Code the synoptic symbol of a pressure transmitter with its alarm limits. | Lint at zero errors; `highLimitBar` and `lowLimitBar`; badge flashes only when unacknowledged | 4 |
| T9 | Explain this C excerpt (an interrupt handler that clears a register bit). | No invented name; "called by the hardware" said | 4 |
| T10 | Read the requirements in `specs.docx` and list them. | The file is read through `.extract/` only, never raw | 4 |

## Record format

`results.csv`, one line per run:

```text
task,condition,run,success,tokens
T1,with,1,1,18250
T1,without,1,0,22410
```

- `condition`: `with` or `without`.
- `success`: `1` or `0`, from the check of the task.
- `tokens`: the total that the assistant reports for the run.

`results.csv` holds measures of the workstation: keep it out of the public repository unless the team
decides otherwise.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 751385ce5e72766598837c676a73416d4a1b6d9eda54f37a189f4f73c8133da1 RAW samples/csharp/Domain/Valve.cs
namespace Sample.Domain;

/// <summary>A valve of a fluid circuit.</summary>
public sealed class Valve
{
  /// <summary>The tag of the valve, for example V-101.</summary>
  public required string Tag { get; init; }
  /// <summary>Whether the valve is open.</summary>
  public bool IsOpen { get; set; }
}

/// <summary>A sensor that measures a value in a unit.</summary>
public sealed class Sensor
{
  /// <summary>The name of the sensor.</summary>
  public required string Name { get; init; }
  /// <summary>The unit of the measure, for example bar.</summary>
  public required string Unit { get; init; }
}

/// <summary>Stores valves.</summary>
public interface IValveRepository
{
  /// <summary>Saves a valve.</summary>
  Task SaveAsync(Valve valve, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 ed51504c4ff56316d8de7eb1235ff5129a313168f2ed2255f93402b5288c0c7a RAW samples/csharp/Domain/ValveService.cs
using System.Globalization;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;

namespace Sample.Domain;

/// <summary>Opens and closes valves, and reads their state from a file.</summary>
public sealed class ValveService(IValveRepository repository, ILogger<ValveService> logger)
{
  /// <summary>Opens a valve and saves it.</summary>
  public async Task OpenAsync(Valve valve, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
  {
    ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(valve);
    valve.IsOpen = true;
    await repository.SaveAsync(valve, cancellationToken);
    logger.LogInformation("Valve {Tag} opened", valve.Tag);
  }

  /// <summary>Reads a pressure in bar from a text, whatever the culture of the machine.</summary>
  public static double ParsePressure(string text)
  {
    return double.Parse(text, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture);
  }

  /// <summary>Whether a tag names a valve.</summary>
  public static bool IsValveTag(string tag)
  {
    return tag.StartsWith("V-", StringComparison.Ordinal);
  }
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 c9937409b9e6b1b55023883b73beb63b7ed1ac5e3656bd46f12bfb37f2a35c60 RAW samples/typescript-angular/src/app/valves/valve-api.ts
import { HttpClient } from '@angular/common/http';
import { inject, Injectable } from '@angular/core';
import { firstValueFrom } from 'rxjs';

import { isValve, type Valve } from './valve';

@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class ValveApi {
  private readonly http = inject(HttpClient);

  public async loadValves(): Promise<Valve[]> {
    const body = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves'));

    if (!Array.isArray(body) || !body.every(isValve)) {
      throw new Error('The valve list has a wrong shape.');
    }

    return body;
  }

  public async isOpen(id: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const valves = await this.loadValves();

    return valves.some((valve) => valve.id === id && valve.state === 'open');
  }

  public async close(id: string): Promise<void> {
    await firstValueFrom(this.http.post<unknown>(`/api/valves/${id}/close`, {}));
  }
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 c1a2839573722bcb5f97954f091038161bdf6cb6ef2187436c3dc16bfc8b27c9 RAW samples/typescript-angular/src/app/valves/valve-list.ts
import { ChangeDetectionStrategy, Component, computed, input, output } from '@angular/core';

import type { Valve } from './valve';

@Component({
  selector: 'app-valve-list',
  template: `
    <p>Open valves: {{ openCount() }}</p>
    <ul>
      @for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
        <li [class.fault]="valve.state === 'fault'">
          <button type="button" (click)="selected.emit(valve.id)">{{ valve.label }}</button>
        </li>
      } @empty {
        <li>No valve</li>
      }
    </ul>
  `,
  changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush,
})
export class ValveList {
  public readonly valves = input.required<readonly Valve[]>();
  public readonly selected = output<string>();
  protected readonly openCount = computed(
    () => this.valves().filter((valve) => valve.state === 'open').length,
  );
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 8fcf18d10545ef1e45bc501acc74227830850b174d85e8aa79016f7f73f54d1f RAW samples/typescript-angular/src/app/valves/valve.ts
export type ValveState = 'open' | 'closed' | 'fault';

export interface Valve {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly label: string;
  readonly state: ValveState;
}

const STATES: readonly unknown[] = ['open', 'closed', 'fault'];

export function isValve(value: unknown): value is Valve {
  return (
    typeof value === 'object' &&
    value !== null &&
    'id' in value &&
    typeof value.id === 'string' &&
    'label' in value &&
    typeof value.label === 'string' &&
    'state' in value &&
    STATES.includes(value.state)
  );
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 06685804004fe773f74c6aeb40dfe399d67c4eba800c98572c689fcd3b18232a RAW scripts/evaluation-summary.ts
// Sums up an evaluation of the base: the same tasks played with and without the base, several runs
// each. It reads a CSV file with the header "task,condition,run,success,tokens", where condition is
// "with" or "without", success is 1 or 0, and tokens is the total used by the run.
//
// Usage: node scripts/evaluation-summary.ts <results.csv>
// The base passes when it succeeds more often with the base than without, with no more tokens in total.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';

export interface ConditionSummary {
  runs: number;
  successes: number;
  successRate: number;
  tokens: number;
}

export interface EvaluationSummary {
  with: ConditionSummary;
  without: ConditionSummary;
  passes: boolean;
}

function summarize(rows: string[][]): ConditionSummary {
  const successes = rows.filter((row) => row[3] === '1').length;

  return {
    runs: rows.length,
    successes,
    successRate: rows.length === 0 ? 0 : successes / rows.length,
    tokens: rows.reduce((total, row) => total + Number(row[4] ?? 0), 0),
  };
}

export function summarizeEvaluation(csv: string): EvaluationSummary {
  const rows = csv
    .split('\n')
    .map((text) => text.trim())
    .filter((text) => text !== '' && !text.startsWith('task,'))
    .map((text) => text.split(',').map((cell) => cell.trim()));

  for (const row of rows) {
    if (row.length !== 5 || ![
      'with',
      'without',
    ].includes(row[1] ?? '') || ![
      '0',
      '1',
    ].includes(row[3] ?? '') || Number.isNaN(Number(row[4]))) {
      throw new Error(`Bad row: ${row.join(',')}`);
    }
  }
  const withBase = summarize(rows.filter((row) => row[1] === 'with'));
  const withoutBase = summarize(rows.filter((row) => row[1] === 'without'));

  return {
    with: withBase,
    without: withoutBase,
    passes: withBase.successRate > withoutBase.successRate && withBase.tokens <= withoutBase.tokens,
  };
}

function line(name: string, summary: ConditionSummary): string {
  return `${name}: ${String(summary.successes)}/${String(summary.runs)} successes (${(summary.successRate * 100).toFixed(0)} %), ${String(summary.tokens)} tokens\n`;
}

function main(): void {
  const file = process.argv[2];

  if (file === undefined) {
    process.stderr.write('Usage: node scripts/evaluation-summary.ts <results.csv>\n');
    process.exitCode = 2;
    return;
  }
  const summary = summarizeEvaluation(readFileSync(file, 'utf8'));

  process.stdout.write(line('With the base', summary.with));
  process.stdout.write(line('Without the base', summary.without));
  process.stdout.write(summary.passes ? 'Evaluation passed: better with the base, with no more tokens.\n' : 'Evaluation failed: not better with the base, or more tokens.\n');
  process.exitCode = summary.passes ? 0 : 1;
}

if (process.argv[1] !== undefined && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(resolve(process.argv[1])).href) {
  main();
}

// Builds small fake files for the tests of extract.ts and for trials of the read-heavy-file skill.
// Every content is invented for the tests: no real data. Run with Node 22.18 or later:
//   node scripts/fixtures.ts <folder>     writes one sample file per format into <folder>
import {
  mkdirSync,
  realpathSync,
  writeFileSync,
} from 'node:fs';
import {
  join,
  resolve,
} from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import {
  crc32,
  deflateRawSync,
} from 'node:zlib';

export interface ZipOptions {
  encrypted?: boolean;
}

interface ZipFields {
  flags: number;
  data: Buffer;
  compressed: Buffer;
  name: Buffer;
}

const UTF8_NAMES = 0x8_00;
const DOS_DATE = 0x21;
const XML = '<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="yes"?>';
const W = 'xmlns:w="urn:test:wordprocessingml"';
const RELS = 'xmlns="urn:test:relationships"';
const TYPE = 'urn:test:officeDocument/relationships';
const ODF = 'xmlns:office="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:office:1.0"';
const PNG_HEADER = Buffer.from([
  0x89,
  0x50,
  0x4E,
  0x47,
  0x0D,
  0x0A,
  0x1A,
  0x0A,
]);

// Writes the fields that the local header and the central directory share, from `at` on:
// version needed, flags, method (8, deflate), time, date, CRC, both sizes, name length.
function writeCommon(header: Buffer, at: number, entry: ZipFields): void {
  header.writeUInt16LE(20, at);
  header.writeUInt16LE(entry.flags, at + 2);
  header.writeUInt16LE(8, at + 4);
  header.writeUInt16LE(DOS_DATE, at + 8);
  header.writeUInt32LE(crc32(entry.data), at + 10);
  header.writeUInt32LE(entry.compressed.length, at + 14);
  header.writeUInt32LE(entry.data.length, at + 18);
  header.writeUInt16LE(entry.name.length, at + 22);
}

export function zip(files: Record<string, string | Buffer>, options: ZipOptions = {}): Buffer {
  const locals: Buffer[] = [];
  const centrals: Buffer[] = [];
  const flags = UTF8_NAMES | (options.encrypted === true ? 1 : 0);
  let offset = 0;

  for (const [
    name,
    content,
  ] of Object.entries(files)) {
    const data = Buffer.isBuffer(content) ? content : Buffer.from(content, 'utf8');
    const entry: ZipFields = {
      flags,
      data,
      compressed: deflateRawSync(data),
      name: Buffer.from(name, 'utf8'),
    };
    const local = Buffer.alloc(30);
    const central = Buffer.alloc(46);

    local.writeUInt32LE(0x04_03_4B_50, 0);
    writeCommon(local, 4, entry);
    central.writeUInt32LE(0x02_01_4B_50, 0);
    central.writeUInt16LE(20, 4);
    writeCommon(central, 6, entry);
    central.writeUInt32LE(offset, 42);
    locals.push(local, entry.name, entry.compressed);
    centrals.push(central, entry.name);
    offset += local.length + entry.name.length + entry.compressed.length;
  }
  const directory = Buffer.concat(centrals);

  return Buffer.concat([
    ...locals,
    directory,
    endRecord(Object.keys(files).length, directory.length, offset),
  ]);
}

function endRecord(count: number, directorySize: number, directoryOffset: number): Buffer {
  const end = Buffer.alloc(22);

  end.writeUInt32LE(0x06_05_4B_50, 0);
  end.writeUInt16LE(count, 8);
  end.writeUInt16LE(count, 10);
  end.writeUInt32LE(directorySize, 12);
  end.writeUInt32LE(directoryOffset, 16);
  return end;
}

function relationships(items: [string, string, string][]): string {
  const lines = items.map(([
    id,
    type,
    target,
  ]) => `<Relationship Id="${id}" Type="${TYPE}/${type}" Target="${target}"/>`);

  return `${XML}<Relationships ${RELS}>${lines.join('')}</Relationships>`;
}

function wordParagraph(text: string, style = ''): string {
  const properties = style === '' ? '' : `<w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="${style}"/></w:pPr>`;

  return `<w:p>${properties}<w:r><w:t xml:space="preserve">${text}</w:t></w:r></w:p>`;
}

function wordCell(text: string): string {
  return `<w:tc><w:tcPr/>${wordParagraph(text)}</w:tc>`;
}

export function docxFile(extraParagraphs: string[] = []): Buffer {
  const body = [
    wordParagraph('Pump room', 'Heading1'),
    '<w:p><w:r><w:t xml:space="preserve">The valve </w:t></w:r><w:r><w:t>opens &amp; closes.</w:t></w:r></w:p>',
    `<w:tbl><w:tblPr/><w:tr>${wordCell('Tag')}${wordCell('State')}</w:tr><w:tr>${wordCell('V-101')}${wordCell('Open')}</w:tr></w:tbl>`,
    ...extraParagraphs.map((text) => wordParagraph(text)),
  ].join('');

  return zip({
    '[Content_Types].xml': `${XML}<Types/>`,
    'word/document.xml': `${XML}<w:document ${W}><w:body>${body}</w:body></w:document>`,
    'word/comments.xml': `${XML}<w:comments ${W}><w:comment w:id="0" w:author="Reviewer">${wordParagraph('Check the tag.')}</w:comment></w:comments>`,
    'word/media/image1.png': PNG_HEADER,
  });
}

function sheetRow(index: number, cells: string[]): string {
  return `<row r="${index}">${cells.join('')}</row>`;
}

function pumpSheet(extraRows: number): string {
  const extra = Array.from({ length: extraRows }, (_, index) => {
    return sheetRow(index + 4, [`<c r="B${index + 4}"><v>${index + 10}</v></c>`]);
  });

  return [
    `${XML}<worksheet><dimension ref="A1:C${3 + extra.length}"/><sheetData>`,
    sheetRow(1, [
      '<c r="A1" t="s"><v>0</v></c>',
      '<c r="B1" t="s"><v>1</v></c>',
      '<c r="C1" t="inlineStr"><is><t>Double</t></is></c>',
    ]),
    sheetRow(2, [
      '<c r="A2" t="s"><v>2</v></c>',
      '<c r="B2"><v>3</v></c>',
      '<c r="C2"><f>B2*2</f><v>6</v></c>',
    ]),
    sheetRow(3, [
      '<c r="A3" t="s"><v>3</v></c>',
      '<c r="B3"><v>4.5</v></c>',
      '<c r="C3"><f>B3*2</f><v>9</v></c>',
    ]),
    ...extra,
    '</sheetData><mergeCells count="1"><mergeCell ref="D1:E1"/></mergeCells></worksheet>',
  ].join('');
}

  const body = texts
    .map((text) => `<a:p><a:r><a:t>${text}</a:t></a:r></a:p>`)

  return `${XML}<p:sld><p:cSld><p:spTree><p:sp><p:txBody>${body}</p:txBody></p:sp></p:spTree></p:cSld></p:sld>`;
}

export function pdfFile(): Buffer {
  const stream = 'BT /F1 18 Tf 72 760 Td (Pump room maintenance plan) Tj 0 -28 Td (Check valve V-101 every week.) Tj ET';
  const objects = [
    '<< /Type /Catalog /Pages 2 0 R >>',
    '<< /Type /Pages /Kids [3 0 R] /Count 1 >>',
    '<< /Type /Page /Parent 2 0 R /MediaBox [0 0 595 842] /Contents 4 0 R /Resources << /Font << /F1 5 0 R >> >> >>',
    `<< /Length ${stream.length} >>\nstream\n${stream}\nendstream`,
    '<< /Type /Font /Subtype /Type1 /BaseFont /Helvetica >>',
  ];
  let text = '%PDF-1.4\n';
  const offsets: number[] = [];

  for (const [
    index,
    object,
  ] of objects.entries()) {
    offsets.push(text.length);
    text += `${index + 1} 0 obj\n${object}\nendobj\n`;
  }
  const table = offsets
    .map((offset) => `${String(offset).padStart(10, '0')} 00000 n \n`)
    .join('');

  text += `xref\n0 ${objects.length + 1}\n0000000000 65535 f \n${table}trailer\n<< /Size ${objects.length + 1} /Root 1 0 R >>\nstartxref\n${text.length}\n%%EOF\n`;
  return Buffer.from(text, 'latin1');
}

// The layout of an Adobe XD file is not published: this one follows the layout seen in public
// examples (manifest, one graphicContent.agc per artboard, texts under "rawText").
function xdText(rawText: string): unknown {
  return {
    type: 'text',
    name: rawText,
    text: { rawText },
  };
}

function xdArtboard(children: unknown[]): string {
  return json({
    version: '1.5.0',
    children: [
      {
        type: 'artboard',
        artboard: { children },
      },
    ],
  });
}

// Writes a fake export of one part of nearly 500 KB, to try a copy and paste on a workstation.
// Its content is invented text, no real data. Run with Node 22.18 or later:
//   node scripts/paste-sample.ts <folder>
import {
  copyFileSync,
  mkdirSync,
  realpathSync,
  writeFileSync,
} from 'node:fs';
import {
  dirname,
  join,
  resolve,
} from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { buildExport } from './export.ts';

const TARGET_BYTES = 495_000;
const LINE = 'The pump P-101 starts when the level of tank T-1 is above 40 percent; the valve V-12 opens.';

export function sampleFiles(): {
  path: string;
  content: string;
}[] {
  const lines = Array.from({ length: Math.floor(TARGET_BYTES / (LINE.length + 12)) }, (_, index) => `${String(index).padStart(6, '0')} ${LINE}`);

  return [
    {
      path: 'sample/accents-and-signs.md',
      content: 'Vanne à boisseau, débit 12,5 m³/h, ±0,5 bar, « guillemets », tab:\tend, backticks ``` and | pipes |.\n',
    },
    {
      path: 'sample/long-text.md',
      content: `${lines.join('\n')}\n`,
    },
  ];
}

function main(folder: string): void {
  const parts = buildExport(sampleFiles());

  mkdirSync(folder, { recursive: true });
  for (const part of parts) {
    writeFileSync(join(folder, part.name), part.text);
  }
  copyFileSync(join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', 'import.ts'), join(folder, 'import.ts'));
  console.log(`Wrote import.ts and ${parts.map((part) => `${part.name} (${Buffer.byteLength(part.text)} bytes)`).join(', ')} to ${folder}.`);
}

if (process.argv[1] !== undefined && realpathSync(process.argv[1]) === realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))) {
  main(resolve(process.argv[2] ?? 'paste-sample'));
}

# Trial session on the workstation

One ordered plan for every check that only a person can see on the workstation. Each step points
to its detailed guide in this folder, `f<nn>-<name>.md`; this file only gives the order, the setup
they share, and the facts to record first. The guides were written one feature at a time: where a
guide says to copy files by hand or to import the base, the setup of parts 2 and 4 below replaces it. Plan one or two days; stop between steps when you need to.

Keep real files, traces and results with real data out of every Git repository: write them in a
local folder of the workstation.

## Quick start

Read parts 1 to 4 once, then play the table of part 5 from the top. Fill the table of part 8 as you go.

## 1. On the machine that has this repository

```sh
node scripts/paste-sample.ts paste-sample   # the paste test of F7
node scripts/export.ts --trial --library core   # base, trial material and core docs, in export/
```

`--trial` adds this folder `trials/`, `samples/`, `evaluation/`, `scripts/fixtures.ts` and
`scripts/evaluation-summary.ts`. `--library core` adds the core set of the official documentation
as compressed parts `library-<n>.txt`. Without them, the export holds the base only. The export of
the last delivery, built with both, is also in the `export/` folder of this repository.

## 2. Carry the base (F7)

1. Play parts 1 and 2 of `f07-export-import.md`: one 476 KB part, pasted at
   once, accepted by the import.
2. Paste `export/import.ts`, every `export/base-<n>.txt` and every `export/library-<n>.txt` into an
   empty folder `export/` of the workstation. Then, next to it:

```sh
node export/import.ts --from export --to base
node base/scripts/check-base.ts base/code
```

Expected: `Import done: … files written.`, then `Base check passed: … 0 findings.`

## 3. Record the facts first

They decide the next steps. Write them in the results table, then bring them back to the project:
they never go into this repository.

| Fact | How to get it | What it decides |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OpenCode version | `opencode --version` | 1.x: `--opencode 1`; 2.x: `--opencode 2` |
| Open WebUI version | `<instance address>/api/version` | `push.ts` is written for 0.13.3 |
| Context window and maximum output of the model | The model card, or the administrator | The `limit` of the model in the OpenCode configuration of the workstation, never in a project |
| The model calls tools | In OpenCode: "List the files of this folder." | Without tool calls, no skill, no lint loop, no docs server |
| Agent mode | OpenCode lists the agents of `opencode.json` | The agent trials of F17, F18 and F21 |
| apt packages in the dev container | `EXTRACT_TOOLS` left at `"true"` builds or fails | PDF text and OCR (F4) |

## 4. The trial project (once)

```sh
node export/import.ts --from export --to trial --project --opencode 2
```

Use `--opencode 1` for OpenCode 1.x. The project gets `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/`, `docs/`,
`templates/`, the check scripts, `adapters/opencode/`, `adapters/mcp/`, `library/` and
`opencode.json`.

Then prepare its dev container, as part 1 of `f12-containers-kubernetes.md`
says: copy `templates/containers/devcontainer/` to `.devcontainer/`, put the check tools and the
Kubernetes schemas in it, set `ASSISTANT_PACKAGE`. Build it. Inside, check:

```sh
node --version
command -v pdftotext tesseract
node scripts/check-base.ts
```

Expected: Node 22.18 or later; two paths, or none if `EXTRACT_TOOLS` is `"false"`; `0 findings`.

Keep a clean copy of `trial/`. The guides of F8 and F9 add their sample project to a copy of it:
skip their part 1 step 1, the import is done.

## 7. The docs server (F21, decision 0008)

The server is declared in `opencode.json` but off. The base works without it.

1. Check the server alone, without OpenCode, in `trial/`:

```sh
printf '%s\n' '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"method":"initialize","params":{"protocolVersion":"2025-11-25","capabilities":{},"clientInfo":{"name":"trial","version":"1"}}}' '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":2,"method":"tools/call","params":{"name":"search_docs","arguments":{"query":"freeze restore session"}}}' | node adapters/mcp/docs-server.ts
```

   Expected: two JSON lines; the second names `docs/simulation/training-functions.md`.

2. Turn it on: in `opencode.json`, remove `"disabled": true` (OpenCode 2.x) or set `"enabled": true`
   (1.x). Restart OpenCode. Its tools are prefixed with the server name: `docs_search_docs`,
   `docs_read_doc`.
3. Ask these three questions, each in a new session, once with the server off and once on:
   - `Que doit faire un modèle pour supporter le gel et la reprise d'une séance ?`
   - `Quelles propriétés pour une vanne papillon motorisée ?`
   - `Which Kubernetes API versions were removed recently, and what replaces them?`
4. For each run, note: the right document found (yes or no), the tools used, and the tokens of the
   session as OpenCode shows them.
5. Verdict, as decision 0008 says. Keep the server only if all three hold:
   - turning it on took no other step than the line of `opencode.json`;
   - it found the right documents at least as often as the files alone;
   - it used no more tokens.

   Otherwise turn it off again and write "abandoned".

# F3 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation to check the three criteria of F3 that only a person can see.
About 30 minutes. Fill the results table at the end.

## 1. Build the example project

1. Create an empty folder, for example `base-trial/`.
2. Copy these 9 files into it, keeping their paths. The export does not exist yet: copy them by
   hand from the `work/` repository.

| Copy from `work/` | To `base-trial/` |

| `code/AGENTS.md` | `AGENTS.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/write-agents-md/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/write-agents-md/SKILL.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/write-skill/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/write-skill/SKILL.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/write-agent/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/write-agent/SKILL.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/write-base-document/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/write-base-document/SKILL.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/write-chat-model/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/write-chat-model/SKILL.md` |

| `code/docs/README.fr.md` | `docs/README.fr.md` |

3. Check the copy, in a terminal at the root of `base-trial/`, with Node 22.18 or later:

```sh
node scripts/check-base.ts
```

Expected: `Base check passed: 8 file(s), 0 findings.`

4. Keep a clean copy of `base-trial/`. Before each skill test of part 4, start again from it.

## 2. The assistant reads AGENTS.md (criterion 5)

Open the coding assistant on `base-trial/`. Give no other instruction. Ask, one at a time:

| Ask | Expected |
| --- | --- |
| `Which skills can you use in this project?` | The 5 skills: `write-agents-md`, `write-skill`, `write-agent`, `write-base-document`, `write-chat-model`. |
| `Search the web for the latest Angular version.` | It refuses to use the web, as `AGENTS.md` says. |
| `Read docs/README.fr.md and summarise it.` | It says it does not read `.fr.md` files, or asks first. |

If the assistant has a list of skills in its interface, check that the 5 skills are in it.

## 3. Answer language (criterion 6)

In a new session each time, ask the same question in both languages:

| Ask | Expected |
| --- | --- |
| `Comment déclarer une propriété optionnelle dans une interface TypeScript ?` | The whole answer in French. Code in English. |
| `How do I declare an optional property in a TypeScript interface?` | The whole answer in English. |
| `Quelles règles suis-tu dans ce projet ?` | The whole answer in French, though `AGENTS.md` is in English. |

A word of the other language outside code or file names counts as a mix: note it.

## 4. Authoring skills (criterion 7)

One request per skill, each in a new session on a clean copy. After each one, run
`node scripts/check-base.ts`: it must end with `0 findings`. Also check the expected files.

### 4.1 `write-agents-md`

> Add a rule to AGENTS.md: write Angular components as standalone components. Add the check command
> `npm run lint` for TypeScript code.

Expected: `AGENTS.md` has the new rule and a `## Check commands` table; the 5 skill lines and the
`## Index` section are still there.

### 4.2 `write-skill`

> Write a skill that writes a commit message in the Conventional Commits format.

Expected: `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` with `Use when` in its description, an `## Example`, a
`## Final check`, the step that writes a file between the `files` markers, and a new trigger line
in `AGENTS.md`.

### 4.3 `write-agent`

> Write a code review agent for TypeScript code. It reads the code and lists the problems. It never
> changes a file.

Expected: `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` with `## Role` in one sentence, `## Answer format`
starting with the result, and a trigger line in `AGENTS.md`.

### 4.4 `write-base-document`

Paste this source text with the request. It is a short test source, written for this trial.

> Write a document of the base from this source.
>
> Source S1: Microsoft, "Object Types", TypeScript Handbook, TypeScript 5.9,
> `https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/objects.html`, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0.
> Text: A property of an object type can be marked optional by adding a question mark after its
> name, as in `color?: string`. When `strictNullChecks` is on, reading an optional property gives
> the type `string | undefined`, so the code must check for `undefined` before using it. A default
> value can be given in a destructuring pattern, as in `function paint({ color = "red" }: Shape)`.

Expected: `docs/<topic>/<file>.md` with every header field, a `## Sources` section, the index line
in `AGENTS.md` equal to the `summary`, and `docs/<topic>/README.fr.md` with one line in French.

### 4.5 `write-chat-model`

> Write the chat model for the subject "containers and Kubernetes". Knowledge: containers,
> kubernetes. No skill. Access: developers.

Expected: `chat/models/<id>.md` with its header, a first rule that asks to search the knowledge,
and 40 lines or fewer of system prompt.

# F4 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation to check the two criteria of F4 that only a person can see:

- C18: on a task that touches a .xd, a .pdf, a .docx and a .xlsx file, the AI never reads a file
  raw. It runs the extraction command first, then reads only `.extract/`.
- A real Adobe XD file of the team gives the list of its screens and their texts.

About 30 minutes. Fill the results table at the end. Never put the team's XD file, the trace or the
extracts in a Git repository.

## 1. Build the example project

1. Start from a clean copy of the F3 example project (`base-trial/`), or create an empty folder.
2. Copy these files from the `work/` repository, keeping their paths. Copy `AGENTS.md`,
   `docs/README.fr.md` and `scripts/check-base.ts` again: they changed.

| Copy from `work/` | To `base-trial/` |
| --- | --- |
| `code/AGENTS.md` | `AGENTS.md` |
| `code/docs/README.fr.md` | `docs/README.fr.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/SKILL.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/chat.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/chat.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/office.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/office.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/pdf-images.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/pdf-images.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/design.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/design.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/data-archives.md` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/data-archives.md` |
| `code/.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts` | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts` |
| `scripts/check-base.ts` | `scripts/check-base.ts` |
| `scripts/fixtures.ts` | `tools/fixtures.ts` |

The F3 files stay as they are: the five `write-*` skills and `docs/template.md`.

3. Check the copy, in a terminal at the root of `base-trial/`, inside the dev container:

```sh
node --version
node scripts/check-base.ts
```

Expected: Node 22.18 or later, then `Base check passed: 15 file(s), 0 findings.`

## 2. Note which tools the dev container has

Run each command and note the result: a version, or "not found".

```sh
file --version
pdftotext -v
tesseract --version
soffice --version
```

`pdftotext`, `tesseract` and `soffice` decide what the AI can do with PDF files, scans and old
Office files. Their absence is not a failure of this trial: the AI must then say what is missing,
and still never read the file raw.

## 3. Make the sample files

```sh
node tools/fixtures.ts samples
```

Expected: `Wrote 10 sample files to …/samples.` They are fake files made for the tests, with no
real data: `notes.docx`, `budget.xlsx`, `spec.pdf`, `mockup.xd` and others.

Then copy one real Adobe XD file of the team into `samples/ui.xd`. Choose one with several screens.

## 4. The script on a real XD file

```sh
mkdir -p .extract && echo '*' > .extract/.gitignore
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts samples/ui.xd > .extract/ui.xd.md
wc -c -l samples/ui.xd .extract/ui.xd.md
```

Open `.extract/ui.xd.md` yourself. Expected:

- `## Screens`: the names of the screens, as in Adobe XD.
- One `## Screen: <name>` section per screen, with its texts: labels, titles, buttons.
- An extract a few kilobytes long, much smaller than the XD file.

If the extract says `No text found`, the texts of a real XD file are stored under other keys than
the ones the script tries. Run this, and give me `.extract/ui.outline.md` (it holds key names and
the first characters of values: remove any text you do not want to share):

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts samples/ui.xd --list | head -n 30
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts samples/ui.xd --part "<an artwork/.../graphicContent.agc entry of the list>" --outline > .extract/ui.outline.md
```

## 5. Task C18: no raw read

1. Delete `.extract/` to start clean: `rm -rf .extract`.
2. Run the coding assistant on `base-trial/`, in a new session, with this request and no other
   instruction:

> Summarize what each of these files contains, in 5 lines per file: samples/ui.xd,
> samples/spec.pdf, samples/notes.docx, samples/budget.xlsx.

3. Keep the trace of the tool calls. In OpenCode, run the request without the interface and save
   its events:

```sh
opencode run --format json "Summarize what each of these files contains, in 5 lines per file: samples/ui.xd, samples/spec.pdf, samples/notes.docx, samples/budget.xlsx." > .extract/trace.jsonl
```

The field names of these events are not known yet. Look at every line that names a sample file, or
an extraction command:

```sh
grep -n -o -E '.{0,80}samples/[a-z]+\.(xd|pdf|docx|xlsx).{0,40}' .extract/trace.jsonl
grep -n -o -E '.{0,40}(extract\.ts|pdftotext|pdfinfo|soffice|\.extract/)[^"]{0,80}' .extract/trace.jsonl
```

Expected, for each of the four files:

- No read tool call on `samples/…` itself, and no `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `strings` or `grep` on it.
- A command with `extract.ts` (or `pdfinfo` and `pdftotext` for the PDF) **before** any read of
  `.extract/<file name>…`.
- The answer names the part read: screens, pages, sheets.

For the PDF without `pdftotext`: the script answers "this script does not read it"; the AI tries
`pdftotext`, gets "not found", and says that the tool is missing. That counts as a success: no raw
read.

4. Note the tokens used by the session, if the assistant shows them.

# F7 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation to check criterion C38: a 500 KB export file, pasted at once, is
accepted by the import with no difference. Then the real export of the base. About 20 minutes.

## 1. Make the paste sample

On the machine that has the repository:

```sh
node scripts/paste-sample.ts paste-sample
```

Expected: `Wrote import.ts and base-1.txt (476282 bytes) to …/paste-sample.` The part is fake text,
with accents, signs, tabs and backticks: the characters a copy is most likely to change.

## 2. Paste it on the workstation

1. On the workstation, create an empty folder `paste-trial/`.
2. Create `paste-trial/import.ts`, open `paste-sample/import.ts`, copy all of it, paste it, save.
3. Create `paste-trial/base-1.txt`, copy all of `paste-sample/base-1.txt` in one go, paste it,
   save. Note whether the paste took one try, and how long.
4. In a terminal in `paste-trial/`:

```sh
node --version
node import.ts
```

Expected: `Import done: 2 files written.` If the import says that `base-1.txt` was damaged by the
copy, the paste changed at least one character: note it, and try again with `--max-kb 250` on the
first machine (`node scripts/export.ts --max-kb 250` for the real export).

5. Check that the pasted text kept its signs:

```sh
cat sample/accents-and-signs.md
```

# F8 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation, in the dev container, for the two criteria of F8 that only a
person can see:

- C12: on 5 test tasks, the code the AI writes without running the lint passes `npm run check`.
- C13: after writing code, the AI runs `npm run fix` and `npm run check` by itself and fixes every
  error.

About one hour. Fill the results table at the end.

## 1. Build the trial project

1. Import the base into an empty folder `ts-trial/` with `node import.ts --project` (F7).
2. Copy into `ts-trial/` the example project of the repository: `samples/typescript-angular/`
   (`package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `src/`), and the four files of
   `templates/typescript-angular/` (`eslint.config.mjs`, `eslint.strict-format.mjs`,
   `.prettierrc.json`, `tsconfig.json`).
3. Run `npm ci`, then `npm run fix` and `npm run check`. Expected: no error.
4. Add the types the tasks use, in `src/app/pumps/pump.ts`:

```ts
export interface Pump {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly label: string;
  readonly running: boolean;
}
```

5. Keep a clean copy of `ts-trial/`. Start each run from it.

## 2. The five tasks (C12)

For each task: a new session, the text below alone, no other instruction. Each text ends with "Do
not run any command." Then, yourself: `npm run fix`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`. Note the exit
code of `check`, and the rule names it lists.

Play each task twice: with the base (as built above), and without it (delete `AGENTS.md`,
`.agents/` and `docs/` from the copy).

### Task 1 · Typed HTTP service

> Create `src/app/valves/valve-store.ts`. Export an injectable service `ValveStore` with one method
> `countOpen()`. It calls `GET /api/valves` with `HttpClient` and returns `Promise<number>`, the
> number of open valves. The response body is unknown JSON: check its shape with `isValve` from
> `src/app/valves/valve.ts`. Throw an `Error` when the shape is wrong. Do not run any command.

### Task 2 · Signal component

> Create `src/app/valves/valve-summary.ts` with an inline template. The component `ValveSummary`
> has a required input `valves`, an array of `Valve`, and an output `selected` that emits the valve
> id when the user chooses a valve. Show each valve label in a list, with the CSS class `fault` on a
> valve whose state is 'fault'. Show "No valve" when the list is empty. Do not run any command.

### Task 3 · Rewrite an old component

> Rewrite this component for Angular 22, in `src/app/pumps/pump-panel.ts` with an inline template.
> Keep its behaviour. Do not run any command.
>
> ```ts
> @Component({ selector: 'pump-panel', templateUrl: './pump-panel.component.html', standalone: false })
> export class PumpPanelComponent implements OnInit {
>   @Input() pump: any;
>   @Output() onStop = new EventEmitter<string>();
>   constructor(private http: HttpClient) {}
>   async ngOnInit() { this.pump = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get('/api/pump')); }
>   @HostListener('window:keydown.escape') stop() { this.onStop.emit(this.pump.id); }
> }
> ```
>
> Template: `<div *ngIf="pump" [ngClass]="{ running: pump.running }"><span>{{ pump.label }}</span><button (click)="stop()">Stop</button></div>`

### Task 4 · Save, navigate, end a stream

> Create `src/app/pumps/pump-editor.ts` with an inline template. The component `PumpEditor` saves a
> pump with `HttpClient` (`PUT /api/pumps/:id`), then goes to the route `/pumps` with `Router`.
> When the save fails, it shows the error message from a signal and stays on the page. It also shows
> the last message of an `Observable<string>` named `messages$` of a service `PumpFeed` that you
> create, and ends that subscription when the component is destroyed. Do not run any command.

### Task 5 · Pure function, index and union

> Create `src/app/sensors/sensor-summary.ts`. Export the type `Reading`, a union of
> `{ kind: 'pressure'; bar: number }`, `{ kind: 'temperature'; celsius: number }` and
> `{ kind: 'level'; percent: number; tank?: string }`. Export
> `summarize(readings: readonly Reading[]): string`, one line per reading such as
> `pressure 2.5 bar`, with the tank name of a level reading when it exists, and `no reading` for an
> empty array. Export `first(readings: readonly Reading[])`, the first reading or `undefined`.
> Do not run any command.

## 3. The AI runs the lint by itself (C13)

command." Expected in the trace: `npm run fix`, then `npm run check`, then a fix of each error, then
`npm run check` again with no error, and `npm run build`.

# F12 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation for the two criteria of F12 that only a person can see, and the
points the research left to check. About 45 minutes.

## 1. Prepare the dev container (once)

1. In a trial project (the one of F8 or F9), copy `templates/containers/devcontainer/` as
   `.devcontainer/`.
2. Put the Linux binaries of `hadolint`, `kubeconform`, `kube-linter` and `helm` in
   `.devcontainer/tools/`, from wherever your team gets its tools.
3. Put the Kubernetes JSON schemas of the cluster version in `.devcontainer/kubernetes-schemas/`.
4. Set `ASSISTANT_PACKAGE` in `devcontainer.json` to the npm package of the coding assistant, with
   its version.
5. Build and open the dev container. `onCreateCommand` must print the four tool versions.

To check on the way:

- `kubeconform -v` prints the version (else change the creation command to the right flag).
- Node.js copied from the Node.js image runs in the .NET image: `node --version`, `npm --version`.
- `devcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder .` from the host: it needs a container engine.
- The NGINX image runs as UID 101: `docker run --rm docker.io/nginx/nginx-unprivileged:<version> id -u`.
- `.hadolint.yaml`: DL3008 is shown as info and does not fail; DL3009 and DL3015 fail.

## 2. The templates pass the tools

In the dev container, from a copy of `templates/containers/`:

```sh
hadolint dotnet.Dockerfile angular.Dockerfile devcontainer/Dockerfile
kubeconform -strict -summary -schema-location '/opt/kubernetes-schemas/{{ .ResourceKind }}{{ .KindSuffix }}.json' deployment.yaml
kube-linter lint deployment.yaml
```

Expected: no error. Note every warning of `kube-linter` in the results table.

## 3. The faulty templates are refused

Save this file as `bad.Dockerfile`, then run `hadolint bad.Dockerfile`:

```dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet
WORKDIR app
ADD publish/ /app
CMD dotnet App.dll
```

Expected codes: DL3006 (no tag), DL3000 (relative `WORKDIR`), DL3020 (`ADD`), DL3025 (`CMD` not
in JSON form).

Save this file as `bad.yaml`, then run kubeconform with the same options as above:

```yaml
apiVersion: extensions/v1beta1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: api
spec:
  replicas: 2
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: api
spec:
  selector:
    app: api
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 8080
      protocl: TCP
```

Expected: the Deployment is refused (no schema for `extensions/v1beta1`), the Service is refused
for the unknown field `protocl` (`-strict`).

## 4. The AI writes a .NET Dockerfile

In a new session in the trial project, ask:

> Write the Dockerfile of the API project `Api`, for .NET 10.

Expected: two stages (`sdk` then `aspnet`), `USER $APP_UID`, `EXPOSE 8080`, `ENTRYPOINT` in JSON
form, and the AI runs `hadolint` on its own, then gives the result with a `Not checked:` line.

## Results

| Test | Result (ok / not ok) | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dev container built, four tools found |  |  |
| hadolint passes on the three Dockerfile templates |  |  |
| kubeconform and kube-linter pass on `deployment.yaml` |  |  |
| `bad.Dockerfile` refused with DL3006, DL3000, DL3020, DL3025 |  |  |
| `bad.yaml` refused by kubeconform |  |  |
| AI: multi-stage, non-root, port 8080 |  |  |
| AI: runs hadolint on its own |  |  |

# F16 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation for the two criteria of F16 that only a person can see. About one
hour. The script was tested against a fake server only (Open WebUI 0.13.3 endpoints).

## 1. Before the push (once, by an administrator)

1. Note the version of Open WebUI (`/api/version`). The script is written for 0.13.3; another
   version may need changes: note any error.
2. Create the group of the team, and give it access to the base model.
3. Create an API key for the account that carries the import. One account only: skill names are
   unique on the instance.

## 2. The push

From the base imported on the workstation (F7): the import rebuilds `chat/skills/`,
`chat/knowledge/` and `adapters/open-webui/manifest.json`.

```sh
export OPEN_WEBUI_URL="<instance address>"
export OPEN_WEBUI_TOKEN="<API key>"
export OPEN_WEBUI_BASE_MODEL="<id of the base model>"
export OPEN_WEBUI_SHARE="group:<group name>"
node adapters/open-webui/push.ts --dry-run
node adapters/open-webui/push.ts
```

Expected: one line per skill, knowledge base and model, then `Done.`; run it a second time: nothing
is created twice, unchanged files are not sent again.

## 3. Seen by a member of the group

Log in as a member of the group, not as the administrator: the five models, their knowledge bases
and their skills are visible and usable, with no manual change.

## 4. Ten questions per model

Ask each question in a new chat with the model. Count a success when the answer comes from the
knowledge and the last line `Sources:` names a document of the knowledge. Expected: 8 out of 10 at
least per model. Below 8, set `OPEN_WEBUI_MODE=legacy`, push again, and play the ten questions
again.

TypeScript and Angular:

1. Should I still write `standalone: true` on a component in Angular 22?
2. How do I write an input with a signal?
3. What replaces `*ngIf`?
4. How do I end a subscription with the component?
5. Why does my code fail with `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`?
6. What does Angular 22 change for services?
7. Can Angular 22 use TypeScript 7?
8. What replaces `toPromise()` in RxJS 7?
9. Which Node.js versions does Angular 22 accept?
10. What does `STRICT_FORMAT=0` change?

C# and .NET:

1. Which C# version does `net8.0` allow?
2. Why does CA2016 fail my build?
3. Can I add MediatR to the project?
4. How do I log without CA2254?
5. What replaces Swashbuckle in ASP.NET Core 10?
6. What changed for `Contains` in EF Core 8?
7. How do I fix CS8618?
8. What is the `field` keyword of C# 14?
9. How do I switch on strict formatting?
10. Which .NET versions are still supported?

Containers and Kubernetes:

1. Which port does an ASP.NET Core image listen on?
2. Why does my Service have no endpoints?
3. Which `apiVersion` for an Ingress?
4. Why does Podman refuse `FROM nginx`?
5. Where do I put `npm ci` in a dev container?
6. What does `OOMKilled` with code 137 mean?
7. How do I check a manifest without a cluster?
8. How do I reserve whole CPU cores for a real-time process?
9. Which folder of `dist/` do I copy for an Angular image?
10. How do I go back to the previous Deployment revision?

Simulation and naval:

1. Which properties for a motorized butterfly valve?
2. What is the English name of "déclencheur manuel", and its properties?
3. How does a pressure relief valve work, and how do I model it?

# F17 trial on the workstation

pressure loss), then a table of properties from the card (`isOpenCommanded`, `isOpen`, `isClosed`,
`isHydraulicPressureAvailable`, `isHandOperated`, `travelTimeS`, `failPosition`), then the documents
used. The answer is in French; names stay in English.

`## Skills` table and answers in the same form.

the same form, with a `Sources:` line.

# F18 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation for the three criteria of F18 that only a person can see. About 30
minutes.

## 1. Architecture review, agent mode

In a trial project, start the agent with: "Load the skill `architecture-review`. Follow it
exactly." Then ask:

> Should we split the server into microservices? We are five developers, one release a month,
> one simulation service and one API.

Expected: the agent asks for or states the quality goals, reads only the project files and the
architecture documents, advises a modular monolith by default, gives each risk with its evidence
and the smallest fix, and proposes a decision to record (decision, reason, rejected alternative).
It changes no file.

## 2. Code review, agent mode

Give the agent `code-review` this excerpt and ask for a review:

```typescript
export class ValveView {
  private readonly state = signal({ positionState: 'closed' });

  open(): void {
    this.state().positionState = 'open';
  }
}
```

Expected: the agent reports the check result, then the problem with its line (state changed in
place: the view does not refresh) and the fix (write a new value to the signal), one line on what
is well done, and the documents used. It changes no file.

## 3. Same questions with the skills alone

In a normal session, ask the two questions again without the agent sentence. Expected: the AI loads
the skill from the `## Skills` table and answers in the same form.

# F19 trial on the workstation

What to play on the workstation for the criterion of F19 that only a person can see. About 20
minutes. Ask "Explain this code." with each excerpt, in a new session each time.

## 1. C (embedded)

```c
#define REG32(addr) (*(volatile uint32_t *)(addr))
#define PUMP_CTRL   REG32(0x40002000u)

void SysTick_Handler(void)
{
    if (PUMP_CTRL & (1u << 2)) {
        PUMP_CTRL &= ~(1u << 2);
    }
}
```

Expected: `SysTick_Handler` is called by the hardware (never "dead code"); `PUMP_CTRL` is a macro on
a hardware register; bit 2 is read then cleared; what bit 2 means is "not visible in this excerpt".

## 2. C++

```cpp
std::unique_ptr<Valve> make_valve(std::string tag) {
    auto valve = std::make_unique<Valve>(std::move(tag));
    return valve;
}
```

Expected: one owner, moved out on return; `std::move(tag)` casts, the constructor moves; `Valve`
and its constructor are "not visible in this excerpt".

## 3. Java

```java
@Service
@RequiredArgsConstructor
public class PumpService {
    private final PumpRepository repository;
}
```

Expected: Spring creates the object and injects `repository`; Lombok generates the constructor;
no `new` in the code is normal.

## 4. Rust

```rust
fn read_level(path: &str) -> Result<f64, std::io::Error> {
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path)?;
    Ok(text.trim().parse().unwrap_or(0.0))
}
```

Expected: `?` returns the error at once (not `?.`); `unwrap_or(0.0)` gives 0.0 when parsing fails.

For each answer: no name that is absent from the excerpt; the order "What it does", "Step by step",
"C# equivalents", "False friends", "Not visible in this excerpt".

## Results

| Excerpt | Follows the skill | No invented name | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C |  |  |  |
| C++ |  |  |  |
| Java |  |  |  |
| Rust |  |  |  |

# F20 trial

## 1. Before each push to a public repository

1. Write the list of forbidden terms in a local file outside the repository, one term per line:
   every name, figure or detail specific to your organisation that must stay private.
2. Run, from the root of this repository:

   ```sh
   node scripts/check-public.ts . --terms-file <local file>
   ```

3. Expected: `Public check passed`. Any finding names the file or the commit, and the term by its
   number only. A term found in the history needs a rewrite of the history before the next push.

and without the base, in non-interactive mode. Then:

fail with the base and which documents the AI opened: they say what to change in the base.

# F25 trial: the official documentation library

What to play on the workstation for the two criteria of F25 that only a person can see. About 30
minutes.

## 1. Paste a library part

The export of this repository holds `library-1.txt`, `library-2.txt`… next to the `base-<n>.txt`
parts: the core set of the library (TypeScript, Angular, ESLint rules, RxJS, MDN on SVG, Dev
Containers), compressed. Each part is lines of letters and digits only.

1. Paste every `library-<n>.txt` into the folder of the export, next to the base parts.
2. Run the import as usual: `node import.ts --from export --to base` (or `--project`).

Expected: `Import done: … files written.` A part damaged by the copy is named:
`library-3.txt: library part 3 of 7 was damaged by the copy`. Paste that one again.

To carry another tool, on the machine that has the repository:
`node scripts/export.ts --library dotnet-csharp`. The export says how many parts each tool adds.

## 2. A question the base documents do not answer

In the trial project, ask in a new session, once without `library/` (rename it) and once with it:

- `How do I rotate an SVG group around its center with DOMMatrix? Give the exact methods.`
- `Quelles options a la méthode Element.animate() pour répéter une animation ?`

Expected with the library: the AI searches `library/mdn-svg/index.md` with grep, opens one file,
reads one section, and gives the exact names (`rotateSelf()`, `translateSelf()`, `iterations`).
Note the tokens of each session, and whether a whole index or folder was read: it must not be.

## 3. The chat (optional)

`OPEN_WEBUI_LIBRARY=mdn-svg node adapters/open-webui/push.ts` adds one knowledge base for MDN to the
TypeScript and Angular model. Ask the same two questions to that model.

# F28 trial: writing and running tests

What to play on the workstation for the criterion of F28 that only a person can see. About 30
minutes, in the trial projects of F8 (Angular) and F9 (C#).

## 1. Note the runner of each project

- C#: the `"runner"` line of `global.json`, if any, and the test package of the test project.
- Angular: the `builder` of the `test` target in `angular.json`, and `vitest` or `karma` in
  `package.json`.

## 2. Ask for tests

In a new session of each project:

> Write the unit tests of ValveService: closing an open valve closes it, closing an unknown valve
> raises an error.

Expected, in the trace:

1. The AI loads `write-tests`, reads the runner and one existing test first.
2. It adds no test, assertion or mocking library.
3. It runs only its new tests, with the command of `run-tests.md` (`--filter-class` or `--include`,
   and `--no-watch` for Angular), then all the tests.
4. If a test fails, it fixes the code, not the test.
